Lanjutkan ke **“Cheatsheet MyBatis Code Generation & Mapper Governance: MyBatis Generator, Dynamic SQL Support Generation, Naming Convention, Generated vs Handwritten Mapper, Regeneration Strategy, dan PR Quality Gate”** karena setelah DSL dipahami, langkah enterprise berikutnya adalah mengelola mapper yang di-generate dan handwritten agar tidak drift, tidak sulit direview, dan tetap production-safe.

---

**Sources:**

- [MyBatis Dynamic SQL](https://mybatis.org/mybatis-dynamic-sql/docs/introduction.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## Response:
7/15/2026, 2:34:03 PM

Lanjut ke code generation dan mapper governance: fokusnya membedakan generated mapper vs handwritten mapper, strategi regenerasi, naming convention, dan quality gate agar mapper tidak drift atau sulit direview.

# Cheatsheet - MyBatis Code Generation & Mapper Governance: MBG, Dynamic SQL Support Generation, Generated vs Handwritten Mapper, Regeneration Strategy, dan PR Quality Gate

## 1. Introduction

**MyBatis Generator / MBG** adalah code generator resmi untuk MyBatis. MBG melakukan introspection ke table database lalu menghasilkan artifact Java/Kotlin/MyBatis untuk operasi CRUD dasar. Dokumentasi resminya juga menegaskan bahwa join query dan stored procedure tetap perlu di-hand-code, jadi MBG sebaiknya dipakai untuk **boilerplate CRUD dan table metamodel**, bukan menggantikan desain query enterprise. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/))

Core governance-nya:

```text
Generated code:
  boleh diregenerate
  jangan diedit manual sembarangan
  cocok untuk CRUD, model, DynamicSqlSupport, mapper dasar

Handwritten code:
  business query
  CTE
  PostgreSQL RETURNING
  procedure/function call
  tenant-aware query
  optimized report/search
  repository orchestration
```

---

## 2. Mental Model

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    A[PostgreSQL Schema] --> B[MyBatis Generator]
    B --> C[Generated Model / Record]
    B --> D[Generated DynamicSqlSupport]
    B --> E[Generated Mapper Interface]

    C --> F[Repository]
    D --> F
    E --> F

    F --> G[Handwritten Query Logic]
    G --> H[Application Service]

    I[Handwritten XML / Annotation Mapper] --> F
    J[CTE / RETURNING / Procedure / Report Query] --> I

    K[Governance Rules] --> B
    K --> I
    K --> F
```

**Rule utama:**

```text
Generated mapper is infrastructure.
Repository is application persistence boundary.
Do not expose generated mapper directly to resource/service unless intentionally simple.
```

---

## 3. Generated vs Handwritten Decision Table

| Kebutuhan | Generated | Handwritten |
|---|---:|---:|
| CRUD simple by primary key | Ya | Biasanya tidak perlu |
| Dynamic SQL support table/column model | Ya | Tidak perlu manual kecuali custom |
| Basic insert/select/update/delete | Ya | Bisa override jika butuh rule khusus |
| Tenant-aware search | Sebagian | Ya, di repository/query method |
| CTE query | Tidak ideal | Ya |
| PostgreSQL `RETURNING` | Terbatas/escape hatch | Ya |
| Stored procedure / PL/pgSQL call | Tidak | Ya |
| Complex join | Tidak | Ya |
| Report/dashboard query | Tidak | Ya |
| Outbox claim with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` | Tidak | Ya |
| Authorization-aware projection | Tidak | Ya |
| Generated table model for DSL | Ya | Ya dipakai oleh handwritten repository |
| JSONB custom query | Sebagian | Biasanya handwritten |
| Performance-tuned SQL | Tidak | Ya |

---

## 4. Target Runtime

MBG memakai `targetRuntime` pada `<context>` untuk menentukan gaya code generation. Dokumentasi MBG menyebut default target runtime adalah **`MyBatis3DynamicSql`**, yang menghasilkan code yang bergantung pada MyBatis Dynamic SQL library. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/))

| Runtime | Output | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| `MyBatis3DynamicSql` | Java model/record, mapper interface, DynamicSqlSupport, tanpa XML | Recommended modern Java |
| `MyBatis3` | Model, mapper, optional XML, Example class | Legacy; lebih banyak code |
| `MyBatis3Simple` | Model + mapper CRUD sederhana | Cocok jika tidak butuh advanced generated query |
| `MyBatis3Kotlin` | Kotlin data class + mapper + support class | Untuk Kotlin project |

MBG docs menyatakan runtime `MyBatis3DynamicSQL` menghasilkan model/record, mapper interface CRUD berbasis Dynamic SQL, dan support class sebagai metamodel column/table; untuk runtime `MyBatis3`, “by example” functionality disebut brittle dan menghasilkan banyak code, sehingga docs merekomendasikan pindah ke `MyBatis3DynamicSQL`. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/configreference/table.html))

---

## 5. Generated Objects untuk `MyBatis3DynamicSQL`

Untuk setiap table yang di-introspect, MBG `MyBatis3DynamicSQL` menghasilkan tiga object utama:

```text
1. Record/model class
2. Dynamic SQL support class
3. Mapper interface
```

Dokumentasi MBG juga menyebut untuk runtime ini: **tidak ada XML**, **tidak ada Example class**, model selalu flat, dan MyBatis Dynamic SQL library wajib tersedia. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/generatedobjects/dynamicSql.html))

Contoh output:

```text
generated/
  com.company.app.generated.model/
    CaseRecord.java

  com.company.app.generated.mapper/
    CaseDynamicSqlSupport.java
    CaseMapper.java
```

---

## 6. Maven Plugin Setup

MBG menyediakan Maven plugin dengan goal `mybatis-generator:generate`. Dokumentasi Maven plugin menunjukkan versi contoh **2.0.0**, goal `generate`, command `mvn mybatis-generator:generate`, dan opsi `-Dmybatis.generator.overwrite=true` untuk overwrite generated Java files. Plugin juga bisa di-bind ke phase `generate-sources`, sehingga code generated ikut build sebelum compile. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/running/runningWithMaven.html))

```xml
<build>
  <plugins>
    <plugin>
      <groupId>org.mybatis.generator</groupId>
      <artifactId>mybatis-generator-maven-plugin</artifactId>
      <version>2.0.0</version>
      <configuration>
        <configurationFile>
          src/main/resources/generator/generatorConfig.xml
        </configurationFile>
        <overwrite>true</overwrite>
        <verbose>true</verbose>
      </configuration>
      <dependencies>
        <dependency>
          <groupId>org.postgresql</groupId>
          <artifactId>postgresql</artifactId>
          <version>${postgresql.version}</version>
        </dependency>
      </dependencies>
    </plugin>
  </plugins>
</build>
```

Run manual:

```bash
mvn mybatis-generator:generate
```

Run overwrite:

```bash
mvn -Dmybatis.generator.overwrite=true mybatis-generator:generate
```

---

## 7. CLI Setup

Quick start MBG menunjukkan command-line pattern seperti ini:

```bash
java -jar mybatis-generator-core-x.x.x.jar \
  -configfile /path/to/generatorConfig.xml \
  -overwrite
```

Jika `-overwrite` tidak dipakai dan ada conflict, MBG akan menyimpan file baru dengan nama unik seperti `MyClass.java.1`. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/quickstart.html))

**Enterprise recommendation:**

```text
Local developer:
  boleh run manual untuk update generated code

CI:
  bisa verify generated code up-to-date

Production build:
  hindari generate dari live DB
  generate dari controlled schema/migration state
```

---

# 8. `generatorConfig.xml` Baseline - PostgreSQL + Dynamic SQL

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<!DOCTYPE generatorConfiguration
  PUBLIC "-//mybatis.org//DTD MyBatis Generator Configuration 1.0//EN"
  "https://mybatis.org/dtd/mybatis-generator-config_1_0.dtd">

<generatorConfiguration>

  <properties resource="generator/generator.properties"/>

  <classPathEntry location="${postgresql.driver.path}"/>

  <context id="PostgreSQLDynamicSql"
           targetRuntime="MyBatis3DynamicSql"
           defaultModelType="flat">

    <property name="javaFileEncoding" value="UTF-8"/>

    <commentGenerator>
      <property name="suppressDate" value="true"/>
      <property name="suppressAllComments" value="false"/>
    </commentGenerator>

    <jdbcConnection
        driverClass="org.postgresql.Driver"
        connectionURL="${db.url}"
        userId="${db.username}"
        password="${db.password}">
    </jdbcConnection>

    <javaTypeResolver>
      <property name="forceBigDecimals" value="true"/>
      <property name="useJSR310Types" value="true"/>
    </javaTypeResolver>

    <javaModelGenerator
        targetPackage="com.company.app.generated.model"
        targetProject="target/generated-sources/mybatis">
      <property name="trimStrings" value="true"/>
    </javaModelGenerator>

    <javaClientGenerator
        type="ANNOTATEDMAPPER"
        targetPackage="com.company.app.generated.mapper"
        targetProject="target/generated-sources/mybatis">
    </javaClientGenerator>

    <table schema="public"
           tableName="cases"
           domainObjectName="CaseRecord">
      <property name="dynamicSqlSupportClassName" value="CaseDynamicSqlSupport"/>
      <property name="dynamicSqlTableObjectName" value="caseTable"/>
    </table>

    <table schema="public"
           tableName="outbox_events"
           domainObjectName="OutboxEventRecord">
      <property name="dynamicSqlSupportClassName" value="OutboxDynamicSqlSupport"/>
      <property name="dynamicSqlTableObjectName" value="outboxEventTable"/>
    </table>

  </context>
</generatorConfiguration>
```

`<context>` adalah environment generation yang berisi koneksi database, jenis object yang dihasilkan, dan table yang di-introspect. Multiple `<context>` bisa dipakai dalam satu run untuk database atau parameter generation yang berbeda. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/configreference/context.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 9. Important MBG Config Elements

| Element | Fungsi | Production Note |
|---|---|---|
| `<generatorConfiguration>` | root config | satu file per module/domain lebih rapi |
| `<properties>` | externalize property | jangan commit password production |
| `<classPathEntry>` | JDBC driver path | Maven plugin dependency sering lebih praktis |
| `<context>` | generation environment | pilih `targetRuntime` jelas |
| `<commentGenerator>` | comment behavior | `suppressDate=true` agar diff stabil |
| `<jdbcConnection>` | DB introspection connection | gunakan schema dev/test controlled |
| `<javaTypeResolver>` | DB type → Java type | penting untuk `BigDecimal`, Java Time |
| `<javaModelGenerator>` | package/path model | generated source path terpisah |
| `<javaClientGenerator>` | package/path mapper | generated mapper package terpisah |
| `<sqlMapGenerator>` | XML output | tidak dipakai untuk `MyBatis3DynamicSQL` |
| `<table>` | table selection | explicit table list preferred |
| `<columnOverride>` | override column mapping | JSONB/enum/custom type |
| `<ignoreColumn>` | exclude column | sensitive/generated/computed column |
| `<plugin>` | modify generation | gunakan hati-hati |

---

# 10. `<table>` Governance

`<table>` memilih table database untuk introspection. Untuk `MyBatis3DynamicSQL`, MBG akan menghasilkan model/record, mapper interface CRUD berbasis Dynamic SQL, dan dynamic SQL support class. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/configreference/table.html))

```xml
<table schema="public"
       tableName="cases"
       domainObjectName="CaseRecord">
  <property name="dynamicSqlSupportClassName" value="CaseDynamicSqlSupport"/>
  <property name="dynamicSqlTableObjectName" value="caseTable"/>

  <ignoreColumn column="internal_legacy_blob"/>

  <columnOverride column="payload"
                  javaType="com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode"
                  jdbcType="OTHER"
                  typeHandler="com.company.app.mybatis.JsonNodeJsonbTypeHandler"/>
</table>
```

## Important `<table>` options

| Option | Fungsi | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `schema` | database schema | PostgreSQL biasanya `public` atau app schema |
| `tableName` | table target | explicit list lebih aman dari wildcard |
| `domainObjectName` | nama model generated | gunakan suffix `Record` untuk persistence |
| `mapperName` | nama generated mapper | untuk runtime yang support |
| `delimitIdentifiers` | exact case/delimited identifier | hindari mixed-case table jika bisa |
| `delimitAllColumns` | delimit semua column | jarang perlu; bisa noisy |
| `escapeWildcards` | escape `_`/`%` saat introspection | jika table/schema name punya wildcard char |
| `modelType` | `flat`/`record` etc | Java modern bisa `record` jika cocok |
| `dynamicSqlSupportClassName` | nama support class | bagus untuk governance |
| `dynamicSqlTableObjectName` | nama inner table object | bikin DSL lebih readable |
| `ignoreQualifiersAtRuntime` | jangan include schema/catalog runtime | berguna schema-per-tenant |
| `runtimeSchema` | schema runtime berbeda | hati-hati dengan multi-env |
| `rootClass` | superclass generated model | jarang |
| `rootInterface` | super interface mapper | bisa untuk common generated mapper contract |
| `trimStrings` | trim string | hati-hati, bisa ubah data semantics |

MBG docs juga menyebut `modelOnly=true` dapat membuat MBG hanya menghasilkan model class dan tidak menghasilkan CRUD method/client; ini berguna jika table hanya ingin dipakai sebagai data structure atau result map support. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/configreference/table.html))

---

# 11. `columnOverride` untuk PostgreSQL

## 11.1 JSONB

```xml
<columnOverride column="payload"
                javaType="com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode"
                jdbcType="OTHER"
                typeHandler="com.company.app.mybatis.JsonNodeJsonbTypeHandler"/>
```

## 11.2 Enum sebagai Java enum

```xml
<columnOverride column="status"
                javaType="com.company.app.domain.CaseStatus"
                jdbcType="VARCHAR"
                typeHandler="com.company.app.mybatis.CaseStatusTypeHandler"/>
```

## 11.3 Timestamp

```xml
<columnOverride column="created_at"
                javaType="java.time.Instant"
                jdbcType="TIMESTAMP_WITH_TIMEZONE"/>
```

## 11.4 Numeric money

```xml
<columnOverride column="amount"
                javaType="java.math.BigDecimal"
                jdbcType="NUMERIC"/>
```

**Production rule:**

```text
Generated mapping must match production TypeHandler strategy.

If JSONB/enum/timestamptz mapping is not tested with PostgreSQL,
do not trust generated code blindly.
```

---

# 12. Generated Package Strategy

Recommended:

```text
com.company.app.generated.model
  CaseRecord
  OrderRecord
  OutboxEventRecord

com.company.app.generated.mapper
  CaseMapper
  OrderMapper
  OutboxEventMapper
  CaseDynamicSqlSupport
  OrderDynamicSqlSupport
  OutboxEventDynamicSqlSupport

com.company.app.persistence.repository
  MyBatisCaseRepository
  MyBatisCaseQueryRepository
  MyBatisOutboxRepository

com.company.app.persistence.mapper.custom
  CaseCustomMapper
  OutboxClaimMapper
  CaseProcedureMapper
```

**Rule:**

```text
Generated package:
  machine-owned

Custom package:
  developer-owned

Repository:
  stable boundary consumed by application service
```

---

# 13. Generated Mapper Usage Pattern

## 13.1 Avoid direct use in service for complex use case

Bad:

```java
public class CaseApplicationService {

    private final CaseMapper generatedMapper;

    public CaseResponse approve(ApproveCaseCommand command) {
        generatedMapper.updateByPrimaryKey(...);
        return ...;
    }
}
```

Better:

```java
public class MyBatisCaseRepository implements CaseRepository {

    private final CaseMapper generatedMapper;
    private final CaseCustomMapper customMapper;

    public Optional<CaseRecord> findById(UUID tenantId, UUID caseId) {
        SelectStatementProvider statement =
            select(id, tenantId, caseNumber, status, version, createdAt, updatedAt)
                .from(caseTable)
                .where(CaseDynamicSqlSupport.tenantId, isEqualTo(tenantId))
                .and(CaseDynamicSqlSupport.id, isEqualTo(caseId))
                .and(deletedAt, isNull())
                .build()
                .render(RenderingStrategies.MYBATIS3);

        return generatedMapper.selectOne(statement);
    }

    public int transitionStatus(...) {
        return customMapper.transitionStatus(...);
    }
}
```

## 13.2 Generated mapper as low-level adapter

```text
Application service
  -> Repository interface
    -> MyBatis repository implementation
      -> Generated mapper
      -> Custom mapper
```

This keeps generated API from leaking into business/application layer.

---

# 14. Handwritten Mapper Categories

| Mapper | Content | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `CaseCustomMapper` | optimized command/query | optimistic transition |
| `CaseQueryMapper` | DTO projection/search | queue/search/detail |
| `CaseProcedureMapper` | `CALL` PL/pgSQL | approval procedure |
| `OutboxClaimMapper` | CTE queue claim | `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` |
| `CaseReportMapper` | reporting CTE/window | dashboard |
| `BackfillMapper` | controlled maintenance SQL | chunked migration |
| `AdminCaseMapper` | cross-tenant admin | audited admin search |

**Rule:**

```text
Generated mapper:
  simple table-level operations

Handwritten mapper:
  business intent, performance intent, DB-specific behavior
```

---

# 15. Regeneration Strategy

## 15.1 Do not manually edit generated Java

Recommended header/convention:

```java
/**
 * This file is generated by MyBatis Generator.
 * Do not edit manually.
 */
```

If generated files live under `target/generated-sources`, they are naturally treated as build output. If generated files are committed under `src/generated/java`, enforce “no manual edits” with PR review and regeneration checks.

## 15.2 XML generated special case

For legacy XML generation, MBG marks generated XML elements with `@mbg.generated`; on subsequent runs, elements containing that comment are deleted and replaced, while XML elements without that marker remain untouched. MBG also notes SQL Map XML files are **not generated** for `MyBatis3DynamicSQL`. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/generatedobjects/sqlmap.html))

Governance implication:

```text
Dynamic SQL runtime:
  no generated XML

Legacy XML runtime:
  handwritten XML can coexist only if it avoids @mbg.generated marker
```

---

# 16. Where to Put Generated Sources?

## Option A - Generate into `target/generated-sources`

```xml
<javaModelGenerator
    targetPackage="com.company.app.generated.model"
    targetProject="target/generated-sources/mybatis"/>

<javaClientGenerator
    type="ANNOTATEDMAPPER"
    targetPackage="com.company.app.generated.mapper"
    targetProject="target/generated-sources/mybatis"/>
```

Pros:

```text
- clean source tree
- no generated code in Git
- repeatable if DB/schema snapshot available
```

Cons:

```text
- build depends on generation input
- CI must have schema or generated artifacts
```

## Option B - Commit generated code

```text
src/main/generated/
  java/com/company/app/generated/...
```

Pros:

```text
- build does not need DB introspection
- diff review visible
- reproducible without DB
```

Cons:

```text
- generated diffs can be noisy
- manual edits risk
```

## Enterprise recommendation

```text
If schema source-of-truth is migration files and CI can create DB:
  target/generated-sources is clean.

If organization wants generated diffs reviewed:
  commit generated code, but enforce regeneration quality gate.
```

---

# 17. CI Quality Gate: “Generated Code Up-to-Date”

Pattern:

```bash
mvn -DskipTests generate-sources
git diff --exit-code
```

If diff appears, fail CI:

```text
Generated MyBatis code is out of date.
Run:
  mvn mybatis-generator:generate
Commit generated changes.
```

For committed generated source:

```bash
mvn -Dmybatis.generator.overwrite=true mybatis-generator:generate
git diff --exit-code src/main/generated
```

For target generated source:

```bash
mvn clean generate-sources compile
```

---

# 18. Generator Input Governance

**Never generate from random developer database** for enterprise mapper baseline.

Preferred source:

```text
Migration files
  -> ephemeral PostgreSQL container
    -> run Flyway/Liquibase migrate
      -> run MBG introspection
        -> generated code
```

Pipeline:

```text
1. Start PostgreSQL test container
2. Apply migrations
3. Run MBG
4. Compile generated code
5. Run mapper integration tests
6. Fail if generated code diff is unexpected
```

This ensures generated code follows the same schema that production migration creates.

---

# 19. Maven Profile for Codegen

```xml
<profiles>
  <profile>
    <id>codegen</id>
    <build>
      <plugins>
        <plugin>
          <groupId>org.mybatis.generator</groupId>
          <artifactId>mybatis-generator-maven-plugin</artifactId>
          <version>2.0.0</version>
          <configuration>
            <configurationFile>
              src/main/resources/generator/generatorConfig.xml
            </configurationFile>
            <overwrite>true</overwrite>
          </configuration>
          <dependencies>
            <dependency>
              <groupId>org.postgresql</groupId>
              <artifactId>postgresql</artifactId>
              <version>${postgresql.version}</version>
            </dependency>
          </dependencies>
        </plugin>
      </plugins>
    </build>
  </profile>
</profiles>
```

Run:

```bash
mvn -Pcodegen mybatis-generator:generate
```

---

# 20. Generator Properties

`generator.properties`:

```properties
db.url=jdbc:postgresql://localhost:5432/app_db
db.username=app_codegen
db.password=app_codegen

postgresql.driver.path=/path/to/postgresql.jar
```

Enterprise rule:

```text
Local properties:
  allowed for dev only

CI:
  inject via environment/secret

Production:
  never use production credential for codegen
```

---

# 21. DB Role for Code Generation

```sql
CREATE ROLE app_codegen LOGIN PASSWORD '...';

GRANT CONNECT ON DATABASE app_db TO app_codegen;
GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO app_codegen;

GRANT SELECT ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public TO app_codegen;
```

Depending on database introspection needs, additional metadata access may be required, but avoid using migration owner or runtime app owner casually.

```text
Migration owner:
  DDL privilege

Runtime app:
  DML privilege

Codegen user:
  metadata/read-only enough for introspection
```

---

# 22. Generated Model Naming

Recommended naming:

| Database Table | Generated Model | Generated Mapper | Support Class |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cases` | `CaseRecord` | `CaseMapper` | `CaseDynamicSqlSupport` |
| `orders` | `OrderRecord` | `OrderMapper` | `OrderDynamicSqlSupport` |
| `outbox_events` | `OutboxEventRecord` | `OutboxEventMapper` | `OutboxDynamicSqlSupport` |
| `audit_events` | `AuditEventRecord` | `AuditEventMapper` | `AuditEventDynamicSqlSupport` |

Why `Record` suffix?

```text
- distinguishes persistence row from domain aggregate
- avoids using generated model as API response/domain object
- makes mapping boundary explicit
```

---

# 23. Generated Record vs Domain Model

Bad:

```java
public class CaseApplicationService {
    public CaseRecord approve(CaseRecord record) {
        record.setStatus("APPROVED");
        return record;
    }
}
```

Better:

```java
public class CaseAggregate {
    private CaseId id;
    private CaseStatus status;
    private long version;

    public void approve(ApprovalReason reason) {
        if (status != CaseStatus.SUBMITTED) {
            throw new InvalidCaseTransitionException();
        }

        this.status = CaseStatus.APPROVED;
    }
}
```

Mapper boundary:

```java
public final class CaseRecordMapper {

    public static CaseAggregate toDomain(CaseRecord record) {
        return new CaseAggregate(
            new CaseId(record.getId()),
            CaseStatus.valueOf(record.getStatus()),
            record.getVersion()
        );
    }

    public static CaseRecord toRecord(CaseAggregate aggregate) {
        CaseRecord record = new CaseRecord();
        record.setId(aggregate.id().value());
        record.setStatus(aggregate.status().name());
        record.setVersion(aggregate.version());
        return record;
    }
}
```

---

# 24. Generated Mapper Extension Pattern

## 24.1 Keep generated mapper pure

```java
public interface CaseMapper {
    // generated methods only
}
```

## 24.2 Add custom mapper separately

```java
public interface CaseCustomMapper {

    int transitionStatus(
        @Param("tenantId") UUID tenantId,
        @Param("caseId") UUID caseId,
        @Param("fromStatus") String fromStatus,
        @Param("toStatus") String toStatus,
        @Param("expectedVersion") long expectedVersion
    );
}
```

## 24.3 Repository combines both

```java
public final class MyBatisCaseRepository implements CaseRepository {

    private final CaseMapper generatedMapper;
    private final CaseCustomMapper customMapper;

    public int transitionStatus(...) {
        return customMapper.transitionStatus(...);
    }

    public Optional<CaseRecord> findById(UUID tenantId, UUID caseId) {
        SelectStatementProvider statement = ...;
        return generatedMapper.selectOne(statement);
    }
}
```

**Rule:** do not add handwritten methods into generated mapper file if that file is overwritten.

---

# 25. Plugin Governance

MBG plugins can modify or add generated objects. The official docs say plugins implement `org.mybatis.generator.api.Plugin`, and most plugins should extend `PluginAdapter` and override only needed methods. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/reference/pluggingIn.html))

Use plugins for:

```text
- add generated file header
- add annotations
- customize serialization
- add Lombok annotations if org permits
- rename generated methods
- suppress unwanted methods
```

Avoid plugin overuse for:

```text
- embedding business logic
- generating complex hand-tuned SQL
- replacing repository/service design
- hiding security-critical behavior
```

Plugin governance:

| Rule | Why |
|---|---|
| Plugin must have tests | generated behavior is code |
| Plugin config documented | future maintainers need context |
| Plugin output reviewed | hidden generated changes dangerous |
| No business logic plugin | keep domain logic explicit |
| Version pin plugin | reproducibility |

---

# 26. Generated Dynamic SQL Support Usage

Generated support class provides table and column definitions, used by repository query builders. MBG docs describe support class as table definition plus column definitions used as input for generated mapper code and your handwritten `where` clauses. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/generatedobjects/dynamicSql.html))

Example:

```java
import static com.company.app.generated.mapper.CaseDynamicSqlSupport.*;
import static org.mybatis.dynamic.sql.SqlBuilder.*;

public Optional<CaseRecord> findById(UUID tenantIdValue, UUID caseId) {
    SelectStatementProvider statement =
        select(id, tenantId, caseNumber, status, priority, version)
            .from(caseTable)
            .where(tenantId, isEqualTo(tenantIdValue))
            .and(id, isEqualTo(caseId))
            .build()
            .render(RenderingStrategies.MYBATIS3);

    return caseMapper.selectOne(statement);
}
```

---

# 27. Generated Methods Governance

Typical generated Dynamic SQL mapper methods may include:

```text
count
delete
deleteByPrimaryKey
insert
insertMultiple
insertSelective
selectOne
select
selectDistinct
selectByPrimaryKey
update
updateByPrimaryKey
updateByPrimaryKeySelective
```

Governance rule:

| Method | Allowed Directly? | Guard |
|---|---:|---|
| `selectByPrimaryKey` | Sometimes | must include tenant elsewhere? careful |
| `select` with completer | Yes inside repository | tenant predicate required |
| `insert` | Yes inside repository | validate record |
| `insertSelective` | Yes | ensure defaults |
| `updateByPrimaryKey` | Risky | can bypass tenant/version |
| `updateByPrimaryKeySelective` | Risky | patch guard needed |
| `deleteByPrimaryKey` | Risky | prefer soft delete/custom guard |
| `delete` with completer | High risk | repository-only, strict WHERE |
| `count` | Yes | avoid expensive default count |

**Enterprise recommendation:**

```text
Do not expose generated mapper to application service.
Wrap generated mapper in repository with tenant/version/security rules.
```

---

# 28. Handling Generated Update/Delete Risks

Generated `updateByPrimaryKey` often updates by primary key only. In multi-tenant enterprise apps, command updates usually require:

```text
tenant_id
id
expected version
expected state
```

So prefer handwritten update:

```java
UpdateStatementProvider statement =
    update(caseTable)
        .set(status).equalTo("APPROVED")
        .set(version).equalToConstant("version + 1")
        .set(updatedAt).equalToConstant("now()")
        .where(tenantId, isEqualTo(command.tenantId()))
        .and(id, isEqualTo(command.caseId()))
        .and(status, isEqualTo("SUBMITTED"))
        .and(version, isEqualTo(command.expectedVersion()))
        .build()
        .render(RenderingStrategies.MYBATIS3);

int updated = caseMapper.update(statement);
```

Check:

```java
if (updated != 1) {
    throw new ConcurrentModificationException();
}
```

---

# 29. Generated Code and PostgreSQL `RETURNING`

For PostgreSQL `RETURNING`, prefer handwritten annotation/XML mapper because generated CRUD normally focuses on standard CRUD method shape.

```java
@Select(
    value = """
        UPDATE cases
        SET
          status = 'APPROVED',
          version = version + 1,
          updated_at = now()
        WHERE tenant_id = #{tenantId}
          AND id = #{caseId}
          AND status = 'SUBMITTED'
          AND version = #{expectedVersion}
        RETURNING
          id,
          tenant_id,
          case_number,
          status,
          priority,
          version,
          created_at,
          updated_at
        """,
    affectData = true
)
@ResultMap("CaseRecordResult")
CaseRecord approveReturning(ApproveCaseCommand command);
```

---

# 30. Codegen for Multi-Schema / Multi-Tenant

If generating from one schema but running against another, MBG table properties like `ignoreQualifiersAtRuntime`, `runtimeSchema`, `runtimeCatalog`, and `runtimeTableName` can adjust runtime names. The docs specifically call out `ignoreQualifiersAtRuntime` as useful when the same table name exists in several schemas and you want generated SQL not to include schema/catalog at runtime. ([mybatis.org](https://mybatis.org/generator/configreference/table.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## Schema-per-tenant caution

```xml
<table schema="tenant_template"
       tableName="cases"
       domainObjectName="CaseRecord">
  <property name="ignoreQualifiersAtRuntime" value="true"/>
</table>
```

Runtime:

```java
runtimeMapper.setSearchPath(tenantSchema);
```

Governance:

```text
- tenant schema must be validated/whitelisted
- search_path must be transaction-local
- generated SQL must not hardcode template schema
```

---

# 31. Codegen and Migrations

Recommended lifecycle:

```text
1. Write migration
2. Apply migration to local/test DB
3. Run MBG
4. Review generated diff
5. Update repository/custom mapper if needed
6. Run mapper integration tests
7. Commit migration + generated code + tests together
```

PR should fail if:

```text
- migration changes table but generated model not updated
- generated code changed but no migration included
- custom mapper references column not in generated support
- generated code has manual edits
```

---

# 32. PR Quality Gate Template

```text
MyBatis Generator / Mapper Governance Review

1. What schema/table changed?
2. Which migration introduced the change?
3. Was MBG regenerated from migrated schema?
4. Which generated files changed?
5. Are generated changes expected?
6. Any TypeHandler/columnOverride needed?
7. Any generated update/delete method unsafe for tenant/version?
8. Any custom mapper affected?
9. Any repository mapping affected?
10. Any API/domain model affected?
11. Were mapper integration tests updated?
12. Was generated code manually edited?
13. Does CI verify generated code is up-to-date?
```

---

# 33. Generated Diff Review Checklist

| Diff | Review Question |
|---|---|
| New column in model | Should API expose it? Should domain use it? |
| Column type changed | TypeHandler/Java type still correct? |
| Nullable changed | Java primitive/wrapper safe? |
| Column removed | Any custom SQL still references it? |
| Table renamed | Expand-contract followed? |
| New table | Should repository expose generated mapper? |
| New enum/status column | Domain enum updated? |
| JSONB column | TypeHandler configured? |
| Timestamp column | Instant/timezone mapping tested? |
| Generated mapper changed | Any method signature breaks repository? |

---

# 34. Golden Rules

```text
1. Generated code is not domain model.
2. Generated mapper is not application boundary.
3. Generated CRUD is not automatically production-safe.
4. Every generated destructive method must be wrapped or restricted.
5. Handwritten SQL owns enterprise behavior.
6. Codegen must follow migration, not random DB state.
7. Regeneration must be reproducible.
8. Generated diff must be reviewed.
9. Type mapping must be tested against PostgreSQL.
10. CI should catch generated drift.
```

---

# 35. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Risk |
|---|---|
| Generate from developer’s dirty DB | non-reproducible code |
| Manually edit generated Java | lost on regeneration |
| Generated mapper used directly in resource | boundary leak |
| Generated `deleteByPrimaryKey` used for business delete | hard delete accident |
| Generated `updateByPrimaryKey` without tenant/version | lost update / tenant issue |
| No `columnOverride` for JSONB/enum | runtime mapping bug |
| Generated code not committed but CI lacks DB | build not reproducible |
| Generated code committed but no drift check | stale code |
| Use MBG to avoid thinking about query design | poor performance/security |
| Generate all tables with wildcard | too much surface area |
| No naming convention | confusing model/domain/API boundary |
| Plugin mutates code without tests | hidden generation risk |

---

# 36. Production Checklist

| Checklist | Target |
|---|---:|
| `targetRuntime=MyBatis3DynamicSql` for modern Java | Ya |
| Generated package separated from handwritten package | Ya |
| Generated model uses `Record` suffix | Recommended |
| Generated mapper wrapped by repository | Ya |
| TypeHandlers/columnOverride for JSONB/enum tested | Ya |
| Codegen DB comes from migrations | Ya |
| CI verifies generated code up-to-date | Ya |
| Generated destructive methods not exposed directly | Ya |
| Custom SQL handles CTE/RETURNING/procedure | Ya |
| Generated diff reviewed with migration diff | Ya |
| No manual edits in generated Java | Ya |
| Mapper integration tests pass against PostgreSQL | Ya |
| Multi-tenant/schema runtime naming reviewed | Ya |
| Plugin usage documented and tested | Ya |

---

# 37. Decision Table

| Problem | Recommendation |
|---|---|
| Need CRUD boilerplate | MBG `MyBatis3DynamicSql` |
| Need table/column DSL support | Generate DynamicSqlSupport |
| Need CTE query | Handwritten XML/annotation |
| Need PL/pgSQL procedure | Handwritten callable mapper |
| Need PostgreSQL `RETURNING` | Handwritten annotation/XML |
| Need tenant-safe update | Handwritten DSL/update with tenant/version guard |
| Need generated code reproducibility | Generate from migration-applied test DB |
| Need many model classes | MBG model generation |
| Need custom type mapping | `columnOverride` + TypeHandler |
| Need multi-schema runtime | `ignoreQualifiersAtRuntime` + controlled search_path |
| Need custom generated annotations | MBG plugin, tested |
| Need PR safety | Generated diff quality gate |

---

# 38. Roadmap Belajar Singkat

| Urutan | Materi | Target |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | MBG target runtime | Paham `MyBatis3DynamicSql` vs legacy |
| 2 | generatorConfig.xml | Bisa generate model/mapper/support |
| 3 | Maven plugin | Bisa run codegen reproducible |
| 4 | `<table>` config | Naming, schema, overrides |
| 5 | `columnOverride` | JSONB/enum/timestamp mapping |
| 6 | Generated vs handwritten | Boundary jelas |
| 7 | Repository wrapper | Generated mapper tidak bocor |
| 8 | Regeneration workflow | Migration → generate → test |
| 9 | CI drift check | Generated code up-to-date |
| 10 | PR quality gate | Diff aman direview |
